Page No.# 418/433
BEFORE
HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE MANISH CHOUDHURY
ORDER
21.11.2023
Heard Mr. Joshua Sheqi, learned counsel for the petitioners in the batch of
writ petitions - W.P.[C] no. 146 to 186 of 2021 [hereinafter referred to as ‘the 1st
batch of writ petitions]; Mr. Limawapang, learned counsel for the petitioners in the
batch of writ petitions - W.P.[C] no. 81 to 87 of 2023 [hereinafter referred to as ‘the
2
nd batch of writ petitions’]; Mr. Z.N. Ngullie, learned Central Government Counsel
[CGC] representing the respondent Union of India; Mr. L.T. Sangtam, learned
Additional Advocate General, Nagaland assisted by Mr. V. Zhimomi, learned
Government Advocate, Nagaland for the State respondents; and Mr. Moa Jamir,
learned counsel for the respondent National Highway Infrastructure Development
Corporation Limited [NHIDCL].
2. The issues raised in the 1st batch of writ petitions are common and similarly,
the issues raised in the 2nd batch of writ petitions are also common. The learned
counsel for the parties have submitted the subject-matters of the two batches of
writ petitions are inter-related and the same can be considered together. In view of
such consensus expressed by the learned counsel for the parties stating that
exchange of pleadings are complete, all these writ petitions are taken up for
consideration at the request of the learned counsel for the parties.
3. From the comprehensive affidavit filed on behalf of the respondent Union of
India in the 1st batch of writ petitions, it has emerged that the Ministry of Road
Transport & Highways [MoRT&H], Government of India [GoI] is entrusted with the
task of formulating and administering, in consultation with other Central
Ministries/Departments, State Governments/UT Administrations, organizations and
individuals, policies for Road Transport and National Highways, with a view to
increase mobility and efficiency of the road transport system in the country. The
Roads Wings deals with the development and maintenance of National Highways in

Page No.# 419/433
the country. The development and maintenance of National Highways in the
country is being implemented by the MoRT&H, GoI through its implementing
agencies, namely, Public Works Department [PWD], National Highway Authority of
India [NHAI], National Highways Infrastructure Development Corporation Limited
[NHIDCL] and the Border Roads Organization [BRO]. The MoRT&H, GoI has taken
up development of National Highways, the State roads and other roads in North
Eastern Region under the Special Accelerated Development Programme, North East
[SARDP-NE] Scheme for providing road connectivity to backward and remote areas,
areas of strategic importance, etc. One of such projects undertaken by the
MoRT&H, GoI is the project of 2-laning of [i] Longleng - Changtongya Road; [ii]
Mon – Tamlu - Meranglong Road; [iii] Phek – Pfutsero Road; and [iv] Zunheboto –
Chakabama Road in the State of Nagaland under the SARDP-NE to provide inter-district road connectivity.
4. It is stated at the Bar that the 1st batch of writ petitions is relatable to
Zunheboto – Chakabama Road and the 2nd batch of writ petitions is relatable to
Longleng - Changtongya Road & Mon – Tamlu - Meranglong Road with also areas
falling within Kohima district. The project of 2-lanning in respect of the afore-mentioned 4 [four] roads was sanctioned by the MoRT&H, GoI vide Job no. SARDP-NE/State Roads/NG/PWD/2010-11/173 on 09.12.2010 based on a Detailed Project
Report submitted by the Public Works Department [PWD] [NH], Nagaland.
5. After sanction was accorded, tender processes for awarding the construction
works in respect of the afore-mentioned 4 [four] roads were undertaken and in the
said tender processes, one M/s Maytas-Gayatri [a Joint Venture between M/s
Maytas Infra Limited and M/s Gayatri Projects Limited] [hereinafter also referred to,
at places, as ‘the Contractor’] emerged as the lowest quoted bidder. M/s Maytas-Gayatri [JV] entered into a Contract Agreement with the Chief Engineer, PWD [NH],
Nagaland on 03.02.2011 for execution of the construction works in respect of the
afore-mentioned 4 [four] roads. On being awarded the construction works, M/s
Maytas-Gayatri [JV] proceeded to execute the construction works. In respect of the
construction works of the 4 [four] roads, the PWD [NH], Nagaland is the
implementing agency of the MoRT&H, GoI to supervise and monitor the project
contract for a fee which is paid/to be paid in the form of Agency Charges. Thus,

Page No.# 420/433
the Chief Engineer, PWD [NH], Nagaland has the role of supervision and monitoring
of the construction works of the MoRT&H, GoI as per the MoRT&H’s specifications
and other specific specifications and Codes, as mentioned in the Contract
Agreement. The scheduled date of commencement of the project was 19.02.2011
and the same ought to have been completed by 18.02.2014.
6. Due to escalation in the cost of construction, the Chief Engineer, PWD [NH],
Nagaland [hereinafter also referred to as ‘the respondent no. 5’] prepared a revised
estimate of Rs. 2978.2 crores and forwarded the same to the MoRT&H, GoI on
20.02.2012. The respondent no. 5 vide a letter dated 20.02.2012 had also reported
to the MoRT&H, GoI that several buildings, plantations, terrace field/gardens, etc.
were to be demolished/destroyed during hill cutting and for that purpose, the
respondent no. 5 had submitted cost estimate amounting to Rs. 2316.66 lakhs to
the MoRT&H, GoI. The estimate for damage compensation so sanctioned by the
MoRT&H, GoI on 03.05.2013 was for Rs. 2316.66 lakhs.
7. The petitioners in the 1st batch of writ petitions have claimed themselves to
be owners of plots of land located by the side of Zunheboto – Chakabama Road
whereas the petitioners in the 2nd batch of writ petitions have claimed themselves
to be owners of plots of lands located by the side of Longleng - Changtongya Road,
Mon – Tamlu - Meranglong Road and the areas falling within Kohima district where
the proposed 2-lanning road construction works have taken place.
8. From the affidavit-in-opposition filed on behalf of the State respondents, it
has emerged that during the course of execution of the construction works, several
buildings, plantations, agricultural lands, etc. were damaged for which the first
phase of compensation to the affected properties were assessed/measured, as
provided in the estimate, which came to Rs. 2316.66 lakhs and the same was
submitted to the MoRT&H, GoI vide a letter dated 20.02.2012 by the respondent
no. 5. Thereafter, several representations were received by the respondent no. 5
for inclusion of compensation of genuine properties damaged due to the then
ongoing road construction works. The Commissioner & Secretary to the
Government of Nagaland, Works & Housing Department wrote to [i] the Deputy
Commissioner, Kohima; [ii] the Deputy Commissioner, Mokokchung; [iii] the Deputy

Page No.# 421/433
Commissioner, Longleng; [iv] the Deputy Commissioner, Mon; [v] Deputy
Commissioner, Zunheboto; and [vi] the Deputy Commissioner, Phek on 22.08.2012,
directing them to take initiatives for conducting survey for assessment of other
damages likely to be caused due to the then ongoing construction works of the 4
[four] roads covering their respective districts. By the said letter, the jurisdictional
Deputy Commissioners in each of the said 6 [six] districts were directed to conduct
surveys so that all the damages caused due to construction works could be
assessed. The Deputy Commissioners were also asked to make consultation with
the officers of the PWD [NH], Nagaland. By the letter dated 22.08.2012, the
jurisdictional Deputy Commissioners were requested to make necessary surveys
and assessments so as to enable the State Government to take up the matter with
the MoRT&H, GoI. The respondent no. 5 had, on 27.08.2012, directed the
concerned Executive Engineers of PWD [NH] and PWD [R&B] to carry out the
assessment of damages in consultation with other officials in their respective
jurisdiction in co-ordination with the respective jurisdictional Deputy
Commissioners.
9. As the progress of the construction works of the afore-mentioned 4 [four]
roads were purportedly unsatisfactory, a Public Interest Litigation, PIL no. 07/2013
was instituted by one Sri Ketovi Kappo before the Principal Bench of this Court
seeking a direction to complete the construction works of 2-lanning under the
SARDP–NE at the earliest. The PIL no. 07/2013 was disposed of by an order dated
13.10.2015, by appointing a Commissioner to supervise the execution of the
construction works and also directing for payment of the bills to the Contractor.
10. The order passed in the PIL no. 07/2013 was challenged before the Hon’ble
Supreme Court in Civil Appeal nos. 342 – 343 of 2017 [Union of India vs. Hetovi Kappo
and others] by the Union of India. The Civil Appeals were allowed by the Hon’ble
Supreme Court by an Order dated 11.01.2017. The Hon’ble Supreme Court had
recorded satisfaction to the effect that the PIL initiated by Hetovi Kappo was with
the sole purpose to complete the road project at the earliest. Before the Hon’ble
Supreme Court, it was submitted on behalf of the Union of India that it had already
decided to foreclose the contract with M/s Maytas-Gayatri [JV] [the Contractor] and
to execute the construction works of all the afore-mentioned 4 [four] roads by

Page No.# 422/433
inviting a fresh tenders. In the Order dated 11.01.2017, the Hon’ble Supreme Court
recorded satisfaction to allow the Union of India to re-tender the project, by
initiating a fresh tender processes, by following the contemplated procedure.
Taking note of the assurance given before the Court on behalf of the Union of
India, it was directed that the Union of India as well as the State of Nagaland
should ensure completion of the project, within a period of 3 [three] years and 6
[six] months therefrom. It was further observed that consequences emanating out
of the Contract Agreement dated 03.02.2011, executed between the State of
Nagaland and M/s Maytas-Gayatri [JV] could only be settled in terms of the
arbitration clause incorporated in the said agreement. Taking note of presence of
the arbitration clause in the contract agreement, liberty was granted to M/s
Maytas-Gayatri [JV] to recover its dues [if any] in the first instance from the State
of Nagaland and in case M/s Maytas-Gayatri [JV] was dissatisfied with the payment
made to it, M/s Maytas-Gayatri [JV] would be at liberty to invoke the arbitration
clause to the Contract Agreement dated 03.02.2011. The Order dated 13.10.2015
passed in PIL no. 07/2013 was also set aside by the Hon’ble Supreme Court of
India by the Order dated 11.01.2017. While allowing the civil appeals, it was
further observed that in case the Union of India had any counter-claim, it would be
open to seek the same, in the same manner, as had been permitted to M/s Maytas-Gayatri [JV] in the arbitration proceedings before the arbitral tribunal.
11. In the meantime, the Chief Engineer, PWD [NH], Nagaland wrote to the
Commissioner & Secretary to the Government of Nagaland, Works & Housing
Department on 04.02.2016 informing him that it was observed that damage claims
were randomly submitted without proper verifications/justifications. In order to
arrive at accurate assessments, it was suggested that joint verifications of
damaged properties had to be re-surveyed by the PWD, the Administration, the
Village or Town Council and the affected individuals/parties jointly and it was
thereby requested to inform the respective jurisdictional Deputy Commissioners for
assisting the PWD officials for completing such assignments. Thereafter, the Works
and Housing Department, Government of Nagaland through its Deputy Secretary
wrote to the Deputy Commissioners, that is, [i] the Deputy Commissioner, Kohima;
[ii] the Deputy Commissioner, Mokokchung; [iii] the Deputy Commissioner,
Longleng; [iv] the Deputy Commissioner, Mon; [v] Deputy Commissioner,
Zunheboto; and [vi] the Deputy Commissioner, Phek, on 26.02.2016, to constitute

Page No.# 423/433
a Joint Verification Committee each comprising of representatives of the Village
Council and the officials from the Departments of Land Records, PWD, Agriculture,
Horticulture, Rural Development, Land Resources, Fisheries. Instructions were
given also to involve the affected landowners/individuals in order to arrive at
accurate assessment of damages. In continuation of the letter dated 26.02.2016,
the Works and Housing Department, Government of Nagaland wrote to the said six
Deputy Commissioners again, on 27.02.2017, informing them that the requisite
verification reports of assessment of damages done during ongoing works of 2-
laning of [i] Longleng - Changtongya Road; [ii] Mon – Tamlu - Meranglong Road;
[iii] Phek – Pfutsero Road; and [iv] Zunheboto – Chakabama Road were not
received till then. By referring to the direction of the Hon’ble Supreme Court of
India in the order dated 11.01.2017 [supra], it was conveyed that all outstanding
payments/claims were to be decided through arbitration as per the terms and
conditions of the contract agreement. It was clarified that the damages caused due
to dumping of earth, etc. were to be included in the claims component of
compensation. It was, thus, requested to ensure that the verification reports were
submitted within 15 days of issuance of the letter to enable the Works & Housing
Department, Government of Nagaland to arrive at accurate assessment of
damages.
12. Pursuant to the Order of the Supreme Court, a Meeting was held under the
Chairmanship of Director General [Road Development] & Special Secretary,
MoRT&H, GoI with the officials from the PWD [NH] Nagaland including the
respondent no. 5. Subsequent to the said Meeting, the decision taken therein was
communicated to the Commissioner & Secretary to the Government of Nagaland,
Work & Housing Department [Works Branch], Nagaland, and the respondent no. 5
on 14.02.2017 in the following manner.
a. CE, PWD[NH] Nagaland to submit the details of expenditure
incurred including centages in the project till date through the Regional
Officer, Guwahati for closing the Sanction of the work, PWD RO,
Guwahati to the Ministry.
b. CE, PWD[NH], Nagaland has brought out the damages have
occurred to property/environment etc. due to cutting of hills by the

Page No.# 424/433
contractor and people have claimed compensation. These compensation
amounts can be claimed from the contractor by raising counter claim
against him and will be dealt during Arbitration process.
c. RO, Guwahati is to submit the expenditure details head wise for de-sanction of the work.
13. Subsequent to the letter dated 14.02.2017 of the MoRT&H, GoI, the
respondent no. 5 vide a letter dated 26.07.2017 submitted the details of
assessment to the MoRT&H, GoI as, in the meantime, the jurisdictional Deputy
Commissioners appear to have submitted their respective statement of calculation
of assessment. In the said letter, the respondent no. 5 had mentioned that the
claim for compensation for damages caused during the execution of the project
thereof amounted to Rs. 120,99,81,755/-. The following abstract of assessment of
compensation was forwarded therewith.

14. In the meantime, the Contractor, M/s Maytas-Gayatri [JV] submitted its final
bill amounting to Rs. 1221.28 crores to the respondent no. 5 on 09.03.2017. On
04.09.2017, the respondent no. 5 by its letter of even date responded to M/s

Page No.# 425/433
Maytas-Gayatri [JV] by disputing and discarding the said claim of M/s Maytas-Gayatri [JV]. The respondent no. 5 by the letter dated 04.09.2017 had further
informed that there would be a counter-claim from the end of the State of
Nagaland for the losses suffered by the landowners for damages occurred at the
sites to structures, crops, irrigation and cultivation fields, etc.
15. Such denial of claim led to the institution of arbitral proceedings with the
invocation of the arbitration clause by M/s Maytas-Gayatri [JV]. The Arbitral
Tribunal consisting of 3 [three] Arbitrators announced its Award on 10.08.2019.
Before the Arbitral Tribunal, a counter-claim for an amount of Rs. 121 crores was
raised by the State of Nagaland against M/s Maytas-Gayatri [JV] on the ground of
random dumping of excavated materials which caused loss to the PWD [NH],
Nagaland. The counter-claim for an amount of Rs. 121 crores was, however,
rejected by the Arbitral Tribunal observing that the documents relied on by the
counter-claimants were various abstracts of assessments of cost signed by the
Deputy Commissioners. The Union of India in its comprehensive affidavit, has
mentioned that the Arbitral Tribunal while rejecting the counter-claim by assigning
reason, had acknowledged that the act of dumping of excavated materials on the
roadside by M/s Maytas-Gayatri [JV] might have caused great hardship to the local
public. After the Arbitral Tribunal’s Award dated 10.08.2019, the respondent no. 5
preferred an application before the Arbitral Tribunal requesting it to pass an award
for an additional amount vis-à-vis the counter-claim since the landowners of the
affected areas were aggrieved parties for illegal dumping of excavated materials
that took place during the time of execution of construction works of the highways
by M/s Maytas-Gayatri [JV] which, in turn, had badly damaged poor farmer’s
terrace fields, irrigation canals, crops, orchards, vegetation, plantation, etc. The
Arbitral Tribunal by an Order dated 16.10.2019 had dismissed the application
observing that it had no jurisdiction to pass any additional award.
16. Subsequent to the approval accorded by the MoRT&H, GoI vide letter dated
27.09.2019, the respondent no. 5 has challenged the Award of the Arbitral Tribunal
under Section 34 of the Arbitration and Conciliation Act, 1996 [as amended],
before the Hon’ble Delhi High Court. The MoRT&H, GoI has also filed a separate
petition before the Hon’ble Delhi High Court being OMP[COMM] 25/2020 & IAs

Page No.# 426/433
677-678/2020. It has been submitted at the Bar by the learned counsel for the
parties, in unison, that those matters are pending before the Hon’ble Delhi High
Court, as on date.
17. In the interregnum, the MoRT&H, GoI had, on 14.02.2017, entrusted
supervision of the construction works of the afore-mentioned 4 [four] roads to the
NHIDCL in respect of which the Right of Way [RoW] had already been acquired by
making payment in respect of the landed properties which are located in the RoW
and are affected/likely to be affected due to construction of 2-lanning road
construction works under the SARDP-NE Project.
18. At this stage, it is necessary to refer to another proceedings in the form of a
Public Interest Litigation, PIL no. 09/2019 [Chakhesang Public Organisation and
others vs. The Union of India and others]. The said PIL was filed by Chakhesang
Public Organisation through its functionaries and on behalf of persons whose
lands/properties, including crops, irrigation and fields, drainage, etc. were
damaged due to construction of 2-lane road within Phek District under the 2-lane
Project : [i] Longleng - Changtongya Road; [ii] Mon – Tamlu - Meranglong Road;
[iii] Phek – Pfutsero Road; and [iv] Zunheboto – Chakabama under the SARDP-NE. The PIL was filed praying inter alia for a direction to the respondents to pay
compensations for the damages so caused to the landowners whose lands are
situated adjoining the 2-lane roads but not acquired and damages were caused due
to random dumping of excavated materials. The Division Bench in PIL no. 09/2019
took notice of the situations as to how the damages were caused to the nearby
landowners due to construction works carried out by then contractor, M/s Maytas-Gayatri [JV]. The Division Bench had also arrived at a finding that during the
process of execution of the construction works, a lot of cut-earths, stones, trees,
etc. were piled up along the roads under construction instead of dumping the same
at the identified dumping sites. When the rainy season came, the materials were
slide down on the lands and properties of the villagers who were settled alongside
the road causing severe damages to their lands and properties.
19. The Division Bench had also taken notice that on representation made by
those affected landowners the jurisdictional Deputy Commissioners in consultation

Page No.# 427/433
with the officials of the PWD [NH], Nagaland made assessment of the damages
caused to those landowners and submitted the Statements of Calculation of
damages before the respondent no. 5, as already extracted hereinabove. In the PIL
no. 09/2019, a plea taken by the State respondents was to the effect that the
construction of the said 4 [four] roads under the SADRP-NE project was initiated by
the MoRT&H, GoI and the State of Nagaland was only an Implementing Agency
and thus, the compensation ought to be paid by the MoRT&H, GoI. On the other
hand, the plea raised on behalf of the respondent Union of India was to the effect
that the compensation should either be paid first by the State of Nagaland and
thereafter, make their claims from the Ministry or claim the same in the arbitration
proceedings, which was then pending. Taking note of the respective projections of
the parties, the PIL no. 09/2019 was disposed of by the Order dated 30.01.2023.
The relevant excerpts of the Order dated 30.01.2023 are extracted hereinbelow for
ready reference.
5. Since there is no dispute from the side of the respondents on the
claim of the petitioners that lands and properties of persons whose lands
are situated below the road in question and which are within the
administrative circle of the EAC, Sakraba, Phek District were damaged
or destroyed, the need for a discussion on the claim does not arise.
Record shows that the Project for construction of 2-lane from Pfutsero–
Longleng within which the lands and properties of the farmers or
villagers for whom the writ petition has been filed was initiated and fully
funded by the Central Government and the State Government is only the
implementing agency. Further record shows that compensations have
been paid to the owners of the lands situated above the road which were
affected by the construction of the road. Therefore, it appears that the
problem of paying compensation to the owners of the lands situated
below the road for the damage or destruction caused to their lands and
properties has arisen either due to lack of knowledge about the terrain
along which the road was to be constructed, or ground realities, or
failure to envision the most probable consequences that may follow
during the construction. Had the planners been made aware about all
these, while conceptualising and preparing the final Detailed Project
Report they would have taken the same into account. And the poor land

Page No.# 428/433
owners would not have had to go through all these ordeal of running
from pillar to post for compensation of the loss suffered by them, and no
hindrance on the construction of the road would have been caused at the
same time. When compensation has been paid to owners of the lands
whose lands lie above the road there is no reason why owners of the
lands and properties whose lands lie below the road and are also
affected by construction of the same road should not be entitled to
receive the same. It is true, Right to Property is no longer a fundamental
right but when property of a person is affected or damaged while
undertaking a public related project, and if such person is not
compensated adequately, the consequence is his livelihood is affected
and that would lead to violation of his right to life protected under
Article 21. Therefore, in this case also though the private land owners
whose lands lie below the road constructed have no right to say ‘no’ to
the construction of the road and acquisition of their lands, they have a
right to be compensated adequately for their lands, properties, standing
crops and trees etc. affected by the construction of the road. For to not
do so will violate their rights protected under the said Article. In fact, the
land owners being tribals and marginal land owners, the authorities; be
it from the Central Government or the State Government or both, should
have been a little more sensitive and considerate, if need be, while
undertaking the project so that they need not have gone through such
loss and suffering. The project started in the year 2011; therefore it has
been more than 10 [ten] years since the owners of the lands have
suffered the loss of their lands, crops and properties. A welfare State,
certainly, is not expected to be so apathetic about the genuine grievances
of its own citizens. It does not behove for a welfare state to deny or defer
benefit of such right by giving such excuse as that, the land owners may
make such claim in the pending arbitration process, especially when the
lands owners are not a party in the proceeding. Therefore, to prevent
any further suffering of these people, both the Central Government and
the State Government should act in tandem expeditiously.
Admittedly, the damage caused to the lands and properties have been
already assessed by conducting a joint survey and even the quantum of
the compensation amount to be paid has also been determined under

Page No.# 429/433
the leadership and supervision of the Deputy Commissioner concerned.
As such, the State Government shall make the first move by submitting a
proposal along with the assessment made to the Central
Government/the Ministry concerned, and on receipt of the same the
latter shall initiate the process for allocation of fund and release of the
same to the State Government who then shall disburse the same through
the Deputy Commissioner of the District concerned, to the genuine land
owners whose lands, properties, standing crops and trees etc., have been
damaged or destroyed during the construction of the road. The whole
exercise should be completed within a reasonable time but not beyond 6
[six] months from the date of this judgment and order.
20. This Court on 14.11.2023, had asked the learned Central Government
Counsel and the learned Additional Advocate General, Nagaland to provide a proper
break-up of Rs. 12,99,81,755/- showing how such figure has been reached and the
basis on which the respondent Union of India and the respondent State of
Nagaland authorities had made their counter-claim before the Arbitral Tribunal and
thereafter, have pursued the claim for the said amount in the proceedings,
presently pending before the Hon’ble Delhi High Court.
21. In deference to the Order dated 14.11.2023, Mr. Sangtam, learned
Additional Advocate General, Nagaland has placed a Communication bearing no.
CE[NH]/COURT/LC-14/2023-24 dated 16.11.2023, of the respondent no. 5. The
abstracts enclosed with the said Communication, read as under :-

22. It is apposite to mention that pursuant to the Order dated 30.01.2023
passed in PIL no. 09/2019, the MoRT&H, GoI vide its Order no. NH-12024/03/2023/NG/NE dated 27.10.2023 had conveyed its Administrative Approval,
Technical Approval and Financial Sanction for the payment of award based on the

Page No.# 431/433
Order passed by this Court in PIL no. 09/2019. The damage compensation
amounting Rs. 60,94,36,685/- was sanctioned based on the statement of
calculation of assessment made by the Deputy Commissioner, Phek District for the
areas of land affected during the construction works of 2-lanning on Phek -
Pfutsero Road and Chakabama – Zunheboto Road. The amount sanctioned vide
the Order dated 27.10.2023 tallies with the estimate for Phek-Pfutsero Road and
Chakabama – Zunheboto Road, as shown in the 1st Abstract Table, reproduced
in Para 21 above.
23. From the above fact situation obtaining in the cases in hand, as discussed
above, and from the stands of the respondent authorities in the Union of India and
the State of Nagaland, in the previous proceedings as well as in the counter
affidavits filed in these batch of writ petitions, it is not in doubt that the adjoining
lands of the landowners which are not acquired, were damaged due to random
dumping of excavated materials by the earlier Contractor, M/s Maytas-Gayatri [JV]
whose contract had already been terminated. The claim for an amount of Rs. 121
crores before the Arbitral Tribunal and before the Hon’ble Delhi High Court
had/have been made by the State of Nagaland with the approval of the Union of
India. Such counter-claim had been made with the implied admission that the
damages to that extent had been caused to the landowners whose lands are
situated adjoining to the construction works of the afore-mentioned 4 [four] roads
where 2-lanning construction works were undertaken and are still being
undertaken through the NHIDCL. In such conspectus of facts, it emerges that there
is admission on the parts the respondent authorities in the Union of India and the
State of Nagaland as regards the claims for damages in respect of the areas of land
damaged due to execution of 2-lanning construction of Phek-Pfutsero Road and
Chakabama-Zunheboto Road.
24. The respondent authorities in the Union of India and the State of Nagaland
have not brought anything to rebut the claims made in the two batches of writ
petitions as to why the cases of the landowners of lands adjoining to Longleng -
Changtongya Road and Mon – Tamlu - Meranglong Road with areas within Kohima
District and Chakabama - Zunheboto Road are any different from the cases of the
landowners of areas adjoining to Phek-Pfutsero Road and Chakabama-

Page No.# 432/433
Zunheboto Road in whose favour the amount of Rs. 60,94,36,685/- has already
been sanctioned. This Court finds that the observations recorded by the Division
Bench in PIL no. 09/2019 in the Order dated 30.01.2023 are also fully applicable in
respect of the cases of the landowners of lands adjoining Longlend – Changtongya
Road, Mon – Tamlu – Meranglong Road with areas within Kohima District and
Chakabama – Zunheboto Road. Thus, there is no necessity to repeat the same, for
the sake of brevity.
25. In the cases in hand, that is, in these two batch of writ petitions, the
damages caused to the lands/properties of the landowners having lands adjoining
to [i] Longleng - Changtongya Road; [ii] Mon – Tamlu - Meranglong Road; and [iii]
Zunheboto – Chakabama Road have already been assessed by the respective
jurisdictional Deputy Commissioners and after conducting joint surveys through the
Joint Verification Committee, constituted in terms of the directive of the State
Government in the Works and Housing Department, also submitted their
statements of calculations of assessment of damages before the higher authorities.
It was on that basis such statements of calculations of the jurisdictional Deputy
Commissioners, the respondent Union of India and the respondent State of
Nagaland, have made their claims before the Arbitral Tribunal as well as before the
Hon’ble Delhi High Court. From the proceedings pending as on date, it clearly goes
to establish that there is admission of claim on behalf of the respondent authorities
in the Union of India and the respondent authorities in the State of Nagaland to the
extent of the amounts incorporated in such statements of calculations of
assessment made by the jurisdictional Deputy Commissioners. The reliefs sought
for in these two batches of writ petitions, is no different than the reliefs sought for
in PIL no. 09/2019.
26. After admission of the claims for damages of the landowners, as shown in
the 1st Abstract Table above and sanction of the amount of Rs. 60,94,36,685/-, it is
not open to the respondent authorities in the Union of India and the State of
Nagaland to take a different stance in respect of other Abstract Tables. In view of
the same, the 1st batch of writ petitions as well as the 2nd batch of writ petitions
are disposed of with a direction to the respondent authorities in the MoRT&H, GoI
and the respondent authorities in the State of Nagaland, to initiate the process of

Page No.# 433/433
allocation of fund, in consultation with each other, in terms of the 2nd Abstract
Table and the 3rd Abstract Table, extracted above, provided vide Communication
dated 16.11.2023, and thereafter, to allocate the requisite fund to the jurisdictional
Deputy Commissioners who shall, in turn, disburse the same to the genuine
landowners whose lands/properties, standing corps, etc. found to have been
damaged and destroyed during the execution of the afore-mentioned 4 [four]
roads as the respective statement of calculation. If the names of the petitioners in
these two batches of writ petitions have figured amongst such landowners, they
should be disbursed their respective compensation amounts so assessed by the
jurisdictional Deputy Commissioners. The entire exercise should be completed
within a reasonable time period, but not beyond a period of 6 [six] months from
the date of this order. If any of the petitioners in these two batches of writ petitions
finds himself aggrieved after the process of such disbursement of compensation
amounts due to non-inclusion of his name in the list of beneficiaries, etc. such
petitioner would be at liberty to take recourse to appropriate remedy as permissible
under the law.
27. The Order no. NH-12024/03/2023/NG/NE dated 27.10.2023 of the Ministry
of Road Transport & Highways [NER Zone], Government of India and the
Communication bearing no. CE[NH]/COURT/LC-14/2023-24 dated 16.11.2023 of
the Chief Engineer, PWD[NH], Nagaland, Kohima are kept with the case record of
W.P.[C] no. 146/2021, by marking them as ‘X’ & ‘Y’ respectively.
